# Specification audit 001, follow-up 001: Claude assessment

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Source audit | [Specification audit 001, consolidated report](../../report-final.md) (revision 2, 2026-10-26), 232 findings: 11 High, 82 Medium, 139 Low |
| Follow-up | 001 |
| Reviewer | Claude: orchestrated and written by Claude Opus 5.5; ten verifier agents (Opus 5.5 for safety, durability, trust and architecture batches; Sonnet 5.5 for the bounded spec batches) and two independent Opus 5.5 reviewers |
| Assessment date | 2026-10-29 |
| Audited baseline | `17acb63d9` (the commit the source audit read) |
| Completion baseline | `896e8d8f2` on `main`: every disposition states what holds at this commit |
| Quality baseline | the remediation commits `7a63488a2` (X160) through `809748be8` (X193), and the later product commits to `78d982988` where they touched the same pages or seams |
| Scope | Every source finding: the spec, guidance, skill, test and code change that addressed it, read at the completion baseline; and the quality of that work. No live operation and no command with effects ran. |

This is a fresh assessment of the repository, not an editorial
consolidation. It was written before the reviewer read the parallel
[Codex follow-up](report-codex.md); none of its dispositions or wording
informed this file.

Reading order: [1. Executive summary](#1-executive-summary),
[2. Remaining work](#2-remaining-work),
[3. Quality assessment](#3-quality-assessment),
[4. Implementation plan](#4-implementation-plan),
[5. Verification and limits](#5-verification-and-limits),
[6. Complete findings](#6-complete-findings),
[7. Structured ledger and retained output](#7-structured-ledger-and-retained-output).

## 1. Executive summary

The remediation was real and mostly well made. Of 232 findings, 193 hold at
`896e8d8f2`: 174 closed by a change whose acceptance holds and 19 closed by a
recorded owner decision that the specs state consistently. Ten of the eleven
High findings are closed with a code change, a spec change and a named
negative test. No finding regressed.

| Disposition | High | Medium | Low | Total |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| closed | 10 | 62 | 102 | 174 |
| closed-by-decision | 0 | 10 | 9 | 19 |
| partial | 1 | 10 | 24 | 35 |
| carried by an open item | 0 | 0 | 2 | 2 |
| missing | 0 | 0 | 2 | 2 |
| regressed | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **All** | 11 | 82 | 139 | 232 |

The remediation work itself introduced or left 24 confirmed defects, all Low
but one Medium. Ten duplicate a partial finding and are folded into it
(section 2.2).

Consequential remaining work, in order of consequence:

1. **F17-03 (High, partial).** The Active slices table and its two-way
   checker make authorization decidable, but D168's own text says M2c is
   paused ([backlog.md D168](../../../../../specs/milestones/backlog.md#d168),
   last paragraph) while `specs/milestones/m2.md:231` and the X160 record say
   D168 "pauses no lane", and `specs/milestones.md:33` lists X61 in progress.
   The checker cannot represent a paused slice. The owner must say which
   holds.
2. **F13-02 (Medium, partial) and its new-defect twin.** A root transaction
   inside a running apply or destroy never waits for the root lock
   (`internal/workspace/contextfs/lifecycle_lock_linux_amd64.go:25-35`), so a
   concurrent `status` or `plan` can turn a block `failed` or `unknown`.
   `specs/contexts.md:432-440` states that outcome; no test exercises it and no
   decision accepts it.
3. **F11-08 (Medium, partial, weak).** `specs/security.md:108-111` and
   `specs/cli/commands.md:468-470` say a signed URL never travels on the
   argument vector; `internal/cli/validation.go:395-406` still accepts any
   query on `--from-url`.
4. **Architecture guards weaker than their claim.** The arm-identity rule
   exempts any file whose basename contains `admission`
   (`test/architecture/implementation_identity_test.go:96-97`) and matches
   only exact literals, so later KubeVirt work placed provider-field policy in
   the cluster domain (`internal/containercluster/openshift/pushed_admission.go`)
   and parses the `kubevirt:` endpoint in the power consumer
   (`internal/machine/power/hosted.go:36`, also
   `internal/containercluster/agentinstall/install.go:497,515`). F07-03,
   F07-07 and R2-01-01 remain partial or missing on the same seams.
5. **Silent narrowing.** Several items were closed whole while sub-parts of
   their findings were dropped without a decision or an open item: F14-07
   (change-class checklist), F13-03 (the "build that wrote it" half), F13-08,
   F03-06, F03-09's fingerprint half, F02-01's docs check, F02-06, F17-05,
   F01-11, R2-05-08. This is the most common quality defect and the reason
   most of section 2 exists.

Quality judgment, bounded by the sampling in section 3: the code-bearing
safety lanes (X164 to X170, X176, X178, X180) are good. They change behavior
behind named negative tests that fail without the fix, apply the owner's D183
answers consistently, and fail closed on unreadable facts. The spec-only and
corpus work is adequate: accurate where it edits, but it often narrowed the
recommendation without saying so and left stale sentences on sibling pages.
The extension-architecture work built the mechanisms the audit asked for, but
its guard tests are easier to satisfy than the rule they claim to hold.

### 2.2 Defects in the remediation itself

Confirmed by the independent review; duplicates of a finding above are folded
into it and not listed again (status Next row into F16-08, in-operation lock
into F13-02, restated SANs into F03-03, unrealized table into R2-05-01,
access.unproved sentence into R2-04-04, examples Status into R2-05-08, shell
exceptions into F08-10, B823 into F01-11, page growth into F15-01, section
exceptions into F15-07 and F07-08). Two verifier claims were refuted: the
Active slices lead-in is not circular, and the stale `--authorize` remedy
prints the bound placeholder when unattended (`execution.go:342-348`).

## 3. Quality assessment

Sampling depth: every High and Medium finding had its delivering diff, its
current spec text and its tests read; at least a third of the Lows (most
batches about two thirds) were read the same way. 45 closures from the
Sonnet-verified batches (all 30 High and Medium, 15 sampled Lows) were
re-checked adversarially by an Opus reviewer, who agreed with all 45 and
lowered quality on 7. All 39 non-closed findings and all new defects were
re-checked by a second Opus reviewer, who confirmed every finding, corrected
six remedies and refuted two defect claims. Verifiers ran about 60 named tests
in single packages (section 5); the full gate ran once.

**Implementation.** The safety lanes are the strongest work. X165 made
physical targets prove a durable disk identity in `%pre` (completed by X178's
WWN and serial comparison), made MAC-set proof one equality predicate, refused
power on the controller Machine and unproved targets, and added `bmc-system`
and `bridge-use` claims. X167 refuses retiring storage a surviving cluster
consumes, before registration and before any probe, and the stretch and
tiebreaker work of X198 to X205 did not regress it. X166's credential rebind is
decided per capability in code, so an unknown use refuses. X170's whole-plan
removal, failed-fetch intent clear and fail-closed re-read are sound. X176
moved run scratch under a root-owned parent and made the removal reserve
exact. X163 replaced the audit's "author judgement" with a mechanical guard:
digest recipes, golden mark and proof sections, a literal scan and a
`Contract:` commit line enforced by `check-commits`. Several follow-up lanes
visibly closed windows the first lane left, which shows the parked remainders
were real and were tracked.

**Tests.** Where behavior changed, tests are mostly negative and specific:
forbidden, disconnected and malformed observations; extra MACs; foreign
scratch entries; claims made after registration; retries that exhaust the
reserve; a rotated BMC credential with and without the flag. The docs gates
(ask rules held to the command catalog, record versions held to Go constants,
vendor-support phrases, Active slices, decision order, section budgets,
reachability, kind catalog) each have a seeded-violation fixture. The weak
spots are guards whose scope is narrower than the rule: the arm-identity
filename exemption, the language-row test that sees only collection plugins
and `scripts/`, the unrealized table checked in one direction only, the
remediation test that checks a command's presence but not its exactness, the
untested Stop hook, and the absence of the synthetic second arm and second
storage family that P1-B and P1-C named as exit evidence.

**Maintainability.** The architecture moved in the right direction: one
`substrate_machine` dispatcher, a retirement fan-in port, a neutral storage
evidence port, marker-based discovery of specialization registries, an edge
catalog and a selection closure. Allowlists did not grow:
`implementationIdentityAllowlist` never had a row and `familyKindAllowlist`
went from one to zero with its ceiling lowered. Against that, about twenty Ceph
kind names remain in general-flow diagnostics, cluster preflight imports
storage preflight, and new KubeVirt code reads provider specifics in two
consumers while the tests pass.

**Decisions.** The owner answered Q62 to Q118 in D183, and later D184 to D188.
The specs apply those answers consistently; 19 findings close by decision and
none of them contradicts its page. The defect is the opposite case: changes
narrowed or dropped by the implementing session with no decision, listed in
section 1 item 5. Two recorded claims were wrong in the remediation's own
favor: X160's "M2c is not paused" contradicts D168's text (F17-03), and B823
closed "with no edit" although it also carried F01-11.

**Documentation corrections.** Owning pages were corrected in place, often
across the X171 renames, and Not yet met lines now name an open item: all 47
such lines at the completion baseline name an existing open item, and none of
the 37 audit carriers B800 to B836 is still open. The X171 rename left three
stale references (`architecture.md:1159`, `api/environment.md:186`,
`deferred/machine-arms.md:64-72`), and partial edits left page-to-page
disagreements (`cli.md` against `secrets.md` on `access.unproved`, trust
anchors labelled access credentials, `state-reconciliation.md` against
`add-ons.md` on the GitOps wait set).

**Corpus cost.** The 16 KB milestone cards were not built. The lane
substituted opening budgets and a read-the-row-and-section rule, which serves
the same goal for an agent following AGENTS.md, but recorded no decision, and
m1 to m4 grew 5 to 14 percent (m2 from 141,425 to 161,731 bytes). Ten section
budget exceptions up to 88 KB and 19 naming misfits are ratcheted with no item
that will pay them down.

**Operational limits.** Nothing in this assessment ran against a host. Live
evidence the remediation itself defers stays deferred: B867 (Ceph recapture
identity), B857 (the section 5.7 operator-task run), the libvirt shared-host
items B567 and B568, and every operator acceptance row. Closure here means the
in-tree contract holds; it does not mean live acceptance.

## 4. Implementation plan

The plan authorizes nothing by itself. Each package lands as its own commit
under [code-implementation](../../../../../.agents/skills/code-implementation/SKILL.md),
recorded as a milestone item that its Refs line names. Owner-decision packages
produce a question with options and a recommendation, never an edit that
presumes the answer.

| Package | Findings and defects | Class | Owner decision | Depends on | Exit evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| W1 Owner decision batch | F17-03, F04-05, F04-08, F13-08, F03-06, F03-09, R2-05-08; omitted Ceph `distribution` default; `ceph.md:550` vendor reason (with D203) | owner | yes, all | none | One question per row in the backlog decision table with options and a recommendation; F17-03's answer applied to B484, X61's Active row, `m2.md:231` and the X160 record |
| W2 Signed URL off the argument vector | F11-08 | code, safety | no (applies D183 Q99 a) | none | A usage failure for `--from-url` with a query, naming `--from-url-file`; table test; `make race` and an independent review |
| W3 In-operation root lock wait | F13-02 and its defect twin | code, concurrency | no | none | Bounded, cancellable wait for in-operation root transactions; a test where a held shared lock delays and then admits a block, and one where the bound expires to the stated outcome; `contexts.md` states the bound; `make race`; Opus review |
| W4 Architecture seams and guards | admission exemption, KubeVirt endpoint parsing, F07-07, F07-03, R2-01-01 | code | no | none | Exact exempt-file list with a stale-entry check; `substrate.KubeVirtNamespace`; neutral capacity port; kind-derived diagnostics with the widened family-neutral check; one edge-catalog default and a registry-driven `*Ref` coverage test, each with a seeded violation |
| W5 Custody, trust and remedies | trust-anchor class, R2-04-04, unresolved removal explanation, default remedies with `--context` | code and spec | no | none | Third Secret class in secrets.md and relabelled API rows with the classification test; corrected cli.md and secrets.md rows; a destroy-first explanation test; remedy rendering test |
| W6 Admission and API corrections | R2-05-01, F02-01, F02-06, F03-03 | code | no | none | Two unrealized rows; BMC rewording plus a narrow docs check; "authored add-on" refusal with test and glossary entries; derived `dnsNames` with admission twins and goldens, examples trimmed |
| W7 Spec and guidance corrections | F14-07, F16-08, F18-06, F04-04, F01-11, F13-09, F17-05, F12-04, F05-10, F08-05, F06-12, F17-08, F13-03, F15-01, F15-09, R2-05-07, F02-03, F01-02, F18-02, F04-01 drift, the glossary, `architecture.md:1159`, `api/environment.md:186`, `contexts.md:879-881`, the decision-table cells | spec | no | W4 for architecture.md text | `make docs-check`; each cited line corrected; no new contradiction found by a page-pair grep |
| W8 Gate coverage and ratchet owners | Stop hook test, F15-07, F07-08, F08-10, `e2e/render.sh` row | code and spec | no | none | Stop hook test per branch; a leaf budget that only ratchets; one parked item owning each ratchet (section splits, role and plugin renames, shell logic), cited from the test or reference that holds it; the language-row test covers `e2e/` |

Carried items need no package: F17-07 rides B61 and B755, V2-15 rides B431.

Ordering: W2 and W3 first, because they are the only open safety and
concurrency defects; W4 before W7's architecture.md edits; W1 can go to the
owner at once and blocks nothing else. The role and plugin renames of F07-08
move the automation digest (a build and setup, no destroy), so W8 parks them
rather than doing them in the same change.

## 5. Verification and limits

| Check | Commit | Date | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `make check` in a clean worktree, `GOTMPDIR` and `TMPDIR` under the home cache | `896e8d8f2` | 2026-10-29 | exit 0. `go mod verify`, `tidy -diff`, `go test ./...` (87 packages `ok`, 12 without test files), `go vet` for two targets, the completion test, `govulncheck` and `scripts/ansible-check` all ran. The pytest sessions reported, in order: 571 passed; 4735 passed and 1 skipped; 2311 passed; 7618 passed; 571 passed; 4735 passed and 1 skipped. The controller-tool integration role ended `failed=0`. Retained in 7.2. |
| `make check` in the primary checkout | `896e8d8f2` | 2026-10-29 | not a product result: `fmt-check` stopped at `open examples/wip/m1-rhel-validation/evidence/run-20261009T145605Z-QCeOlt: permission denied`, a root-owned ignored directory; rerun in the clean worktree above |
| Named single-package tests run by verifiers and reviewers | `896e8d8f2` | 2026-10-29 | all passed: `test/architecture` (ask rules, skill discovery, documented bounds, vendor-support lint, milestones, docs gates, unions, nativeError opt-ins, language rows), `api/v1alpha1` (kind catalog, retired table, Ceph kinds), `internal/reconciliation/lifecycle` (resolution, removal, consumer survival, rebind), `internal/environment` (selection closure), CLI diff exit code, catalog spelling, remediation, status parity and readiness, exec session bounds, Python site tests run directly |
| Not-yet-met carrier scan | `896e8d8f2` | 2026-10-29 | 47 lines, each naming an open item; none of B800 to B836 open |

Limits. The skipped case `test_controller_files.py:289` is a skip, not a
pass. `make race` did not run for this documentation-only assessment. No
`ansible-check` suite ran outside the full gate. No live operation, `apply`,
`destroy`, `setup` or command with effects ran. One reviewer used a `cd`
into the pinned worktree for a test run, contrary to its brief; it wrote no
file. Verifier conclusions rest on reading; where a test's behavior was the
evidence, the test was run.

## 6. Complete findings

One row per source finding, in the source catalog order. Step and carrier are the source roadmap step and the parked item X160 created for it. "Delivered by" names the delivered records whose commits did the work. The qualification is the closing evidence for a closed finding and the remaining correction otherwise; the full evidence lists are in the ledger (7.1).

## 7. Structured ledger and retained output

### 7.1 Finding ledger

Generated from the verifier and reviewer results for assessed commit `896e8d8f2` on 2026-10-29. `reviewed_by` names who examined each entry: the batch verifier, the closure review (Opus re-check of Sonnet closures) and the open review (Opus re-check of every non-closed finding and new defect). Paths are repository-relative.

"findings":[

{"id": "F03-03", "severity": "Medium", "section": "S5 Desired-state API", "title": "The grammar makes operators repeat facts and then checks the copies agree", "roadmap_step": "P1-G", "carrier": "B814", "disposition": "partial", "quality": "adequate", "delivered_by": ["X171 b9a0c643b (D183 Q82 b, B333 outcome)"], "open_carrier": null, "evidence": ["specs/api.md:641-648 records the derive-don't-compare principle and names the derived values; specs/api/infrastructure-services.md:79-90 omitted bindAddress derives from the single endpoint address; examples/machines/lab-rhel/infra/componen…", "specs/api/secrets.md:127-134 + internal/secrets/admission.go:43-62 derive generated-certificate dnsNames/ipAddresses from ArtifactServer HTTPS endpoints and emulated-BMC bind address", "specs/ceph-resources.md:694,746 mdsServiceRef optional and derived; node fqdn keeps an explicit override (D183 Q82 b, specs/api/ceph.md:329)"], "remaining": "Derived SANs today come only from endpoint addresses, so a descriptor wanting the controller's DNS name must author both lists (authoring overrides the derivation), which is why lab-rhel restates 192.0.2.1. Derive `dnsNames` too, from the fqdn address of the Machine that serves each consuming ArtifactServer endpoint / emulated BMC (specs/api/secrets.md:127-134, internal/secrets/admission.go), then delete the equal-to-derivation dnsNames/ipAddresses from the example descriptors and regenerate goldens. This satisfies api.md:641-648 without an additive-override contract.", "note": "Derivations for bindAddress, SANs, mdsServiceRef, SMB cluster_id/resourceRefs are real and spec-stated. {"id": "F17-03", "severity": "High", "section": "S9 Spec corpus", "title": "Milestone scope rules are undecidable from tables: no Delivery, stale Next lines", "roadmap_step": "P0-B", "carrier": "B801", "disposition": "partial", "quality": "good", "delivered_by": ["X161 9ef678687"], "open_carrier": null, "evidence": ["specs/milestones.md:27-38 'Active slices' table (slice, milestone, authorized by, state) and :40-48 scope rule 'A slice is active only while its row reads in progress ... at most two active slices per program'", "test/architecture/milestones_test.go:376-440 activeSlices(): row must name an authorizing decision, a planned slice on the right page with an item in progress, per-decision limit of 2, and every in-progress item's slice needs an Active row;…", "backlog.md:3156 Q63 answered by D183: (b) limit per program, standing exceptions retire"], "remaining": "Do NOT add a Delivery column: the Active slices table plus TestMilestones' activeSlices two-way check (every in-progress item's slice has an in-progress row and vice versa, a decision per row, at most 2 per decision) already make authorization decidable from tables, and the per-program WIP rule is the owner's D183 Q63 (b). The real remaining gap is the paused program: D168 says 'M2c is paused' yet X61 (M2c, D159) is listed `in progress` and M2's Next reads \"M2c's X61 (B484)\", and the checker forces any slice holding an in-progress item to read `in progress`, so a paused slice cannot be recorded. After the owner chooses, edit specs/milestones.md (Active slices row and M2 Next), the B484 Delivery cell on m2.md if (b), and, only for (a), add `paused` to the State vocabulary in milestones.md and to activeSlices() with a seeded test case. Reword the lead-in 'Slices with an item `in progress`.' to 'Slices an owner decision authorizes; each holds an item in progress.'", "note": "The Active slices table and two-way checker are well made and make authorization decidable and the WIP rule per program as the owner chose. The audit's literal acceptance (Delivery column on every planned slice, paused vocabulary) was not done and nothing open records that departure.", "reviewed_by": ["verifier", "open-review"], "remedy_class": "owner-decision", "size": "S", "owner_options": "(a) Keep X61 paused: add State `paused` (not authorizing, outside the WIP count) and let the checker accept an in-progress item under a paused row; M2 Next says 'M2c paused (D168)'. (b) Record M2c's pause as B484 Delivery `blocked`, resumption 'the owner resumes M2c (D168)', and drop X61's Active row; no new vocabulary. (c) The owner resumes X61 by a dated decision cited in the row. Recommendation: (b) if the owner confirms the pause (smallest, existing vocabulary); (c) if X61 is in fact live. Also ask the owner to confirm the other five rows (X118, X123, X143, X189, X194) are live authorizations, since the checker can only prove consistency with item cells, not intent."},

{"id": "F15-01", "severity": "Medium", "section": "S9 Spec corpus", "title": "Whole milestone pages, mostly history, dominate every slice task's reading", "roadmap_step": "P2-A", "carrier": "B819", "disposition": "partial", "quality": "adequate", "delivered_by": ["X173 70486c470 (B819)", "X160 7a63488a2 (AGENTS.md/index routing)"], "open_carrier": null, "evidence": ["AGENTS.md:8-10 and specs/index.md:3-7 now mandate the opening, the Planned slices row, the item's row and its ### B<n> section, not the page (the routing half of the recommendation holds)", "test/architecture/docs_test.go:22-42 budgets only the opening of each milestone page (m1 1792, m2 6400, m3 5504, m4 4096 bytes); measured openings 1629/6254/5254/4016 bytes", "Page sizes (wc -c) at HEAD vs audited 17acb63d9: m1 73,195 vs 63,100; m2 161,731 vs 141,425; m3 142,244 vs 128,633; m4 147,544 vs 140,582; so no page is a 16 KB card and every page grew 5-14 percent"], "remaining": "Accept the cheaper design (opening budget + row/section routing in AGENTS.md:8-10 and specs/index.md, enforced by docsByteBudgets) as the closure of the card recommendation, and record that in B819's place (a line in milestones.md Identifiers or the owner batch below). The real remaining work: move dated delivery narrative out of the openings (m2.md:45-90, m3.md:48-59, m4.md:49) into the delivered records or decisions they summarize, then lower the three #opening budgets. Do NOT add a whole-page ratchet: with section routing, page size no longer drives reading cost and a ratchet would block legitimate item additions.", "note": "A cheaper design was chosen (read opening, row and section; budget the opening) and it is enforced, tested and plausible, but it is not what the finding asked for and it was not recorded as a decision. D207); specs/milestones.md:53-57 states the rule", "test/architecture/milestones_decisions_test.go:16-123 requires each decision's status token and that the clauses list names exactly the session-pending entries"], "remaining": "The audit's literal acceptance (the phrase appears only in the list; no waiver rests on a pending clause) is deliberately not met under Q91(a).", "note": "Agree with closed-by-decision; D183 is owner-tagged and the specs state the chosen rule consistently. {"id": "F14-02", "severity": "High", "section": "S10 Skills and harness", "title": "Stop hook runs make quick on the wrong tree and blocks stopping on unrelated failures", "roadmap_step": "P0-A", "carrier": "B800", "disposition": "closed", "quality": "good", "delivered_by": ["X161 9ef678687", "a38a6d183 (original hook)"], "open_carrier": null, "evidence": ["scripts/hooks/stop-check:12-38 exits 0 with '{}' on stop_hook_active, in the primary checkout (git-dir == common-dir), or on a clean tree; :34-46 content key over HEAD, tracked diff and untracked names+content; ok/failed caches in the workt…", ".claude/settings.json:57-67 Stop hook calls \"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR\"/scripts/hooks/stop-check, timeout 300; .codex/hooks.json is tracked and calls the same script", ".agents/skills/code-implementation/references/verification.md:62-76 documents the hook as advisory, command, 300 s timeout, caching"], "remaining": "", "note": "Limit: the hook timeout (300 s) is below focus's own lane bound (900 s), so a slow focus is killed and the hook falls open; acceptable for an advisory hook. {"id": "F07-07", "severity": "Low", "section": "S6 Architecture and specialization", "title": "Preflight and capacity ports are libvirt-only; cluster preflight imports storage", "roadmap_step": "P1-B", "carrier": "B809", "disposition": "missing", "quality": "n/a", "delivered_by": [], "open_carrier": null, "evidence": ["cmd/bootwright/wiring_preflight.go:23 and :66 bind Project: libvirt.CapacityAllocations for storage and cluster preflight", "internal/containercluster/preflight/contracts.go:7 and service.go:15 import internal/storage/preflight", "no backlog/milestone item names an allocation projector (grep)"], "remaining": "Only the cross-domain import is a real defect: move the shared capacity/ownership port types that internal/containercluster/preflight imports from internal/storage/preflight into a neutral package (e.g. internal/machine/capacity) and have both preflights use it. The test proves presence, not exactness.", "reviewed_by": ["verifier"]},
